# Single Step Forward — researched guide drafts

# Chicago, Illinois

Single Step Forward • Source-checked November 18, 2026 • Local review pending

City of Chicago only. Existing guide refresh; do not create a second live Chicago edition.

## Start here

Pick the one need that matters most today. Use the listed route, say where you are and what you need, and ask what happens next. You do not need to solve every problem at once.

For immediate danger or a life-threatening emergency, call 911. For suicide or emotional crisis support, call [988](tel:988?oai_link_source=model_response_hotline), text [988](sms:988?oai_link_source=model_response_hotline), or use the [988 website](https://988lifeline.org?oai_link_source=model_response_hotline). These are US routes.

## Before contacting a service

Ask: “Do you cover my address? Can I apply or be referred now? What documents do I need? Is there a cost or repayment? What should I do if you cannot help?” Mention court, eviction, shutoff or other deadlines early. Ask for an interpreter or accessible contact option when needed.

Share sensitive documents only through the provider’s confirmed intake channel. The listings do not confirm a vacancy, payment, appointment or entitlement. Phone fields are blank when a current number was not specifically verified; use the linked service route.

## Food and essentials

### Greater Chicago Food Depository — Find Food

Search by location and call the listed pantry, meal site, or partner before traveling because schedules and supplies can change.

**Service area:** Chicago & Cook County.

[Open the service page](https://www.chicagosfoodbank.org/find-food-2/) · chicago-015

## Housing and homelessness

### All Chicago — need help

Use the listed shelter or housing access route for your situation. Explain where you can stay tonight and any immediate safety needs. A referral does not guarantee a bed.

**Service area:** City of Chicago; housing programmes have separate eligibility.

[Open the service page](https://allchicago.org/need-help-start-here/) · chicago-004

### National Call Center for Homeless Veterans

Call the national center and provide the veteran's current location and immediate need. Call 911 for immediate danger.

**Service area:** United States.

[Open the service page](https://department.va.gov/homeless/) · chicago-010

## Household bills and benefits

### Illinois ABE benefits portal

Screen or apply for SNAP, cash, and medical benefits, then save the tracking information. Contact the helpline for application questions.

**Service area:** Illinois.

[Open the service page](https://abe.illinois.gov/access/) · chicago-006

### Illinois LIHEAP

Check current application dates and use the official agency locator. Funding, benefit amounts, and required documents can change.

**Service area:** Illinois.

[Open the service page](https://dceo.illinois.gov/communityservices/homeweatherization/communityactionagencies/helpillinoisfamilies.html) · chicago-008

### IRS Free Tax Preparation (VITA/TCE)

Use the official locator during tax season, then call the site to confirm dates, eligibility, appointment rules, and documents.

**Service area:** United States.

[Open the service page](https://www.irs.gov/individuals/free-tax-return-preparation-for-qualifying-taxpayers) · chicago-011

### Veterans Assistance Commission of Cook County

Contact the commission for benefits and emergency-assistance guidance and ask which discharge, identity, income, and dependent documents to bring.

**Service area:** Cook County.

[Open the service page](https://vaccookcounty.org/) · chicago-020

### CEDA — gas and electric bill assistance

Review current utility-assistance instructions and application locations. Confirm documents, funding availability and whether shutoff needs urgent attention.

**Service area:** Cook County; programme eligibility and intake rules apply.

[Open the service page](https://www.cedaorg.net/en/find-services/gas-and-electric) · chicago-023

## Health and dental access

### Get Covered Illinois

Compare coverage, check financial help, and enroll through the official marketplace or request certified enrollment assistance.

**Service area:** Illinois.

[Open the service page](https://getcovered.illinois.gov/) · chicago-007

### Cook County Health — care and financial assistance

Find an appointment or clinic route and ask about financial assistance, dental services and eligibility before care.

**Service area:** Cook County health system; programme and appointment rules vary.

[Open the service page](https://cookcountyhealth.org/) · chicago-022

## Mental health and crisis routes

### SAMHSA — treatment and support finder

Use the official treatment and support tools to locate mental health or substance-use care. Contact the provider about assessment, costs and availability.

**Service area:** United States; provider eligibility and payment rules vary.

[Open the service page](https://www.samhsa.gov/find-help) · chicago-028

## Domestic abuse and safe contact

### Illinois domestic violence support

Use a safe device if possible to review the current contact routes for support and referrals. Website history may remain on the device.

**Service area:** Illinois.

**Phone:** 1-877-863-6338

[Open the service page](https://dvfr.illinois.gov/contact/) · chicago-027

## Legal and consumer support

### Legal Aid Chicago

Use the current intake process and identify any court date or deadline. Help depends on eligibility, issue, conflicts, and capacity.

**Service area:** Chicago.

[Open the service page](https://legalaidchicago.org/get-help/how-do-i-apply-for-legal-aid/) · chicago-001

### Illinois Legal Aid Online

Choose the legal issue and location, then follow the listed program's intake instructions. Online information is not a guarantee of representation.

**Service area:** Illinois.

[Open the service page](https://www.illinoislegalaid.org/) · chicago-009

### Illinois Attorney General Consumer Protection

Read the issue guidance and use the official complaint process. Contact your bank immediately if an account may be compromised.

**Service area:** Illinois.

[Open the service page](https://illinoisattorneygeneral.gov/consumer-protection/) · chicago-012

### CARPLS Cook County Legal Aid Hotline

Call during current intake hours with documents and deadlines ready. Advice or referral availability depends on the issue and capacity.

**Service area:** Cook County.

**Phone:** 312-738-9200

[Open the service page](https://carpls.org/services/cook-county-hotline/) · chicago-016

## Employment and training

### Chicago Cook Workforce Partnership — career services

Use the career-seeker route to find an American Job Center and ask about registration, job-search help or training.

**Service area:** Chicago and suburban Cook County.

[Open the service page](https://chicookworks.org/career-seeker-services/) · chicago-025

## Children and childcare

### Illinois Action for Children — Child Care Assistance Program

Review eligibility and submit the current application with household, work or school, and provider information. Funding and processing times can change.

**Service area:** Cook County.

[Open the service page](https://www.actforchildren.org/child-care/pay-child-care/) · chicago-017

### CEDA — Women, Infants and Children (WIC)

Find a clinic and ask about an eligibility appointment and required documents. Confirm whether the clinic can serve your address.

**Service area:** CEDA WIC clinic network; participant eligibility and service areas apply.

[Open the service page](https://www.cedaorg.net/en/find-services/women-infants-and-children-wic) · chicago-024

## Older people and carers

### Illinois Senior HelpLine

Ask for local support and care-coordination referrals. Describe the person’s location and practical needs; confirm the relevant programme’s requirements.

**Service area:** Illinois; older adults and caregivers.

**Phone:** 1-800-252-8966

[Open the service page](https://ilaging.illinois.gov/resources/helpline-main.html) · chicago-026

## Disability and accessible transport

### CTA reduced-fare programs

Choose the relevant reduced-fare program and confirm eligibility, documents, application steps, and card-processing time.

**Service area:** Chicago transit region.

[Open the service page](https://www.transitchicago.com/reduced-fare-programs/) · chicago-018

### Pace ADA paratransit

Review ADA paratransit eligibility and the certification route. Confirm reservation, fare, service-area and pickup rules before relying on a journey.

**Service area:** Chicago transit region.

[Open the service page](https://www.pacebus.com/paratransit) · chicago-019

## Education and digital access

### City Colleges of Chicago Adult Education

Review adult education options, including English and high-school-equivalency preparation, and ask about placement, dates and enrolment.

**Service area:** Chicago.

[Open the service page](https://www.ccc.edu/site/adult-education/) · chicago-002

### Chicago Public Library community resources

Use the resource guides or find a branch, then confirm hours, computer access, printing, classes, and referral availability.

**Service area:** Chicago.

[Open the service page](https://www.chipublib.org/resources/community-resources/) · chicago-003

## Pet support

### Anti-Cruelty — Pet Resource Center

Check the current pet-food distribution rules before visiting. ID and adult-age requirements apply; Roseland residency changes frequency and food is limited by supplies.

**Service area:** Greater Roseland; pet-food rules also distinguish non-Roseland residents.

[Open the service page](https://anticruelty.org/resourcecenter) · chicago-005

### Anti-Cruelty — pet-owner support directory

Choose pet food, veterinary, housing or emergency boarding support and check that provider’s eligibility, availability and contact instructions.

**Service area:** Chicago-area and linked partner resources; each programme has its own catchment.

[Open the service page](https://anticruelty.org/local-resources) · chicago-031

## Reentry and justice support

### Safer Foundation — reentry education and training

Review the education and training pathways and contact Safer about current enrolment, location and eligibility. Explain any release or work deadline.

**Service area:** Chicago-area services and other Safer locations; programme rules vary.

[Open the service page](https://saferfoundation.org/training-education/) · chicago-029

## Migrant and language access

### ICIRR — immigrant and refugee support

Choose the family-support, benefits or other programme matching your need and find its contact route. Ask about language access and local partner intake.

**Service area:** Illinois; partner agencies have individual catchments.

[Open the service page](https://www.icirr.org/programs-and-services) · chicago-030

## Local emergency and weather information

### Ready Illinois

Review preparedness guidance and use the linked official alert sources for your location. Follow local emergency instructions.

**Service area:** Illinois.

[Open the service page](https://ready.illinois.gov/) · chicago-013

### National Weather Service Chicago

Check the current forecast and active warnings before travel or outdoor plans; follow official protective instructions.

**Service area:** Chicago region.

[Open the service page](https://www.weather.gov/lot/) · chicago-021

## Local service navigation

### 211 Metro Chicago

Call, text, or search for current local services. Confirm hours, service area, and intake directly; call 911 for immediate danger.

**Service area:** Chicago & suburban Cook County.

**Phone:** 211

[Open the service page](https://211metrochicago.org/) · chicago-014

## Review status

The linked service content was checked on the date above. This is a navigation guide, not individual legal or medical advice or a guarantee of help. Providers have not been called to confirm live capacity. National routes are repeated intentionally and are identified by their wider scope. Local reviewers should check catchments, contact options and important omissions in the matching CSV before publication.

This file can be read offline. Opening service pages and completing online applications requires connectivity.

# Barking and Dagenham, Greater London

Single Step Forward • Source-checked November 18, 2026 • Local review pending

Barking and Dagenham local authority only. Shared London or England resources retain their real scope; neighbouring counties are excluded.

## Start here

Pick the one need that matters most today. Use the listed route, say where you are and what you need, and ask what happens next. You do not need to solve every problem at once.

For an emergency, use the [official police emergency contact guidance](https://www.gov.uk/contact-police). For urgent mental health help, use the NHS mental health route below; the service will identify the appropriate local response.

## Before contacting a service

Ask: “Do you cover my address? Can I apply or be referred now? What documents do I need? Is there a cost or repayment? What should I do if you cannot help?” Mention court, eviction, shutoff or other deadlines early. Ask for an interpreter or accessible contact option when needed.

Share sensitive documents only through the provider’s confirmed intake channel. The listings do not confirm a vacancy, payment, appointment or entitlement. Phone fields are blank when a current number was not specifically verified; use the linked service route.

Share sensitive documents only through the provider’s confirmed intake channel. The listings do not confirm a vacancy, payment, appointment or entitlement. Phone fields are blank when a current number was not specifically verified; use the linked service route.

Use the council application route as soon as homelessness is a risk. Explain your situation, immediate safety needs and any deadline; ask what evidence is needed.

Pick the one need that matters most today. Use the listed route, say where you are and what you need, and ask what happens next. You do not need to solve every problem at once.

For an emergency, use the [official police emergency contact guidance](https://www.gov.uk/contact-police). For urgent mental health help, use the NHS mental health route below; the service will identify the appropriate local response.

## Before contacting a service

Ask: “Do you cover my address? Can I apply or be referred now? What documents do I need? Is there a cost or repayment? What should I do if you cannot help?” Mention court, eviction, shutoff or other deadlines early. Ask for an interpreter or accessible contact option when needed.

Share sensitive documents only through the provider’s confirmed intake channel. The listings do not confirm a vacancy, payment, appointment or entitlement. Phone fields are blank when a current number was not specifically verified; use the linked service route.

## Before contacting a service

Ask: “Do you cover my address? Can I apply or be referred now? What documents do I need? Is there a cost or repayment? What should I do if you cannot help?” Mention court, eviction, shutoff or other deadlines early. Ask for an interpreter or accessible contact option when needed.

# Dunedin metropolitan area, New Zealand

Single Step Forward • Source-checked November 18, 2026 • Local review pending

Metropolitan research guide anchored to the Stats NZ 2026 functional urban area. Each listing states its own actual service catchment; this is not a claim that every provider serves the entire metro. Ask the provider to check your address.

## Start here

Pick the one need that matters most today. Use the listed route, say where you are and what you need, and ask what happens next. You do not need to solve every problem at once.

For an emergency response from Police, Fire or Ambulance, call [111](tel:111). For urgent mental health help, use the regional crisis-service contacts in Healthpoint below.

## Before contacting a service

Ask: “Do you cover my address? Can I apply or be referred now? What documents do I need? Is there a cost or repayment? What should I do if you cannot help?” Mention court, eviction, shutoff or other deadlines early. Ask for an interpreter or accessible contact option when needed.

Share sensitive documents only through the provider’s confirmed intake channel. The listings do not confirm a vacancy, payment, appointment or entitlement. Phone fields are blank when a current number was not specifically verified; use the linked service route.

## Food and essentials

### Presbyterian Support Otago — Family Works

Contact Family Works or use its referral form about foodbank, financial mentoring or family support. Confirm how to access the relevant Dunedin service.

**Service area:** Otago; local service and programme rules apply.

[Open the service page](https://psotago.org.nz/contact/family-works/) · nz-dunedin-metro-001

### Work and Income — food help

Check food-assistance eligibility and the application route; ask what evidence is needed and whether support is repayable.

**Service area:** New Zealand.

[Open the service page](https://www.workandincome.govt.nz/eligibility/urgent-costs/food.html) · nz-dunedin-metro-004

## Review status

The linked service content was checked on the date above. This is a navigation guide, not individual legal or medical advice or a guarantee of help. Providers have not been called to confirm live capacity. National routes are repeated intentionally and are identified by their wider scope.
